2026-10-11, about 5:40pm. Asked a fourth time what should come next, the owner chose three of four: daily copies of the library's records, deleted playlists that can be put back, and the phone telling the computer when it forgets the library. (The fourth, the speed leftovers, wasn't picked.) This is the first, and it comes first on purpose: since this afternoon a phone can delete a playlist, and the file all of this lives in had no history.

- **A copy of each day's first version of `state.json`** (`state.py`). Before the first save of a day, the file as it is goes to `.musicorg/backups/state-<day>.json`: playlists, hearts, play counts, listening time and review decisions as they were before that day's first change. The newest seven are kept; an older one is deleted. A day's copy is never written over.
- **The owner's yes was needed, and given:** it's a new place the engine writes. `CLAUDE.md` rule 3 names it under `state.py`, and the library contract has it in the layout and in section 5. The write-rules test already lets `state.py` write its own files, so it didn't change.
- **Written as state.json is** (a temp file, told to the disk, then renamed), so a crash never leaves half a copy under a backup's name. It costs one more save, about a tenth of a second on this iMac, once a day.
- **A copy that can't be made never stops the save.** A full disk, or something in the way of the folder: the records are saved as usual and the log says so, once a day.
- **Nothing reads the copies.** Putting one back is by hand, with the app closed: copy it over `state.json`, then `musicorg index rebuild`. Whether a command or a button should do it is in `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`. They're inside the library folder, so they don't replace Time Machine.
- Taken off `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md`: "state.json has no history" (this), and a line under "For later steps" saying the engine doesn't stop cleanly when the app stops it. It has since step 11: `serve` leaves its loop on SIGTERM, gives the queue's job ten seconds and lets go of the lock, with a test of its own. The list was out of date.
- **Checked:** the engine's tests, all of them (2,179 pass, 5 skipped as ever), and `ruff`. The new ones: the day's first save keeps the file as it was, byte for byte, and no later save that day touches the copy; the next day keeps another; a library's very first save keeps nothing (nothing was there); ten days leave the newest seven, with a stray file and a look-alike in the folder left where they are; a clock put back a year deletes no newer copy; something in the way of the folder stops no save and is said once, and the copy is made at the next save once it's put right; and an engine killed while the copy is being written leaves the records whole and nothing under a backup's name. For real, on the made-up library: two changes made, one copy kept, the same as the file before them by its checksum. **Not done:** a copy put back, by hand or otherwise.

2026-10-11, about 6:05pm. **A deleted playlist can be put back.** The second of the three. Until now a playlist deleted was gone, and since this afternoon a phone can delete one.

The newest fifty are kept. `playlist.restore` puts one back as it was, under the id it had, at the end of the playlists.

2026-10-11, about 6:25pm. **A phone that forgets the library can say so.** The third of the three. Until now its line stayed in Settings → Sync until the owner took it off with Remove.

- **One more thing a device may do, at the owner's choice:** unpair itself. Recorded in `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/ENGINE_API.md` (section 3) beside the other promises. It's only ever itself: the request is known by the key it carries, and whatever else it sends isn't looked at. Nothing of the library changes.
- **Engine** (`sharing`): `POST /sync/v1/forget`, with the device's key. The device is unpaired as Remove does it: its key stops working at once, the app is told (`sharing.changed`) and its line goes. The answer is `{ "forgotten": true }`. No key, a wrong one, or the same request again is 401 `not_paired`. `hello`'s `accepts` ends with `"forget"`, also from an engine that takes no changes (this is the list of devices, not the library).
- It isn't syncing, so it keeps no Mac awake. The same guards as every request: a caller at home, and no web page behind it.
- **App:** nothing new to see. The line goes from Settings → Sync by itself, as a newly paired device's appears.
- **This is the computer's side only**, like the pairing fix it finishes: the phone has to send the request when it forgets a library.
- **Checked:** the engine's tests, all of them (2,184 pass, 5 skipped as ever), and `ruff`: a device unpairs itself and its key is dead; no key, a wrong key, a web page's request and a second go are refused with nothing changed; the other device and the library's records are as they were; the app is told; nothing of the key or an address in the log; and an engine without the library's lock says `["forget"]` and nothing else. **Not tried:** a real phone, and the line going in the app itself (the app's side is the same message that makes a paired device's line appear).

The same address again is still read afresh in its place (however it's written: `stremio://`, capitals in the host's name). Another address is another add-on. So that each can still be asked for, moved, marked and removed on its own:

- An add-on's mark as adults-only stays with its own address. Lists kept before are untouched.

2026-10-11, about 9:30pm. **The count was wrong, and is put right.** The owner ran the first real counts and asked why the seeders and leechers read before pressing Play were hugely different from the ones a minute into playing. The engine's log of their counts (numbers only) showed it at once: "7 with all of it, 2045 others", "4/781", "0/163".

- **What was wrong (mine).** Two things, both in how the numbers were reckoned, not in the torrents:
  - **A count took every peer the engine had merely heard of for a leecher.** In a few seconds it hears of hundreds or thousands of others in a popular torrent and has asked almost none of them what they have, so nearly all landed under "leechers". Whether such a peer has the whole film isn't known until it's been asked.
  - **A film that was open was counted another way:** only those it was connected to. So the same torrent read "7/2045" before and something like "40/5" a minute in.
- **Now one reckoning for both** (`torrents.who_has`): a tracker's own count where the torrent has a tracker and it answers (the figures a torrent's page on the web shows), else those the engine has met, seeds and others apart. A peer only heard of isn't counted. A count now asks the torrent's trackers for their figures as soon as it joins. `torrent.status` gives the same pair for a film that's open (`swarm_seeds`, `swarm_peers`), and the film's page shows that; the player's own line still says who it's fetching from.
- **What will still differ, and why:** for a torrent with no tracker, a count has about eight seconds to meet peers and a playing film has had a minute, so the count is the smaller: "at least this many". The numbers' tip says so. With a tracker's figures the two should agree.
- **Checked:** the engine's tests, all of them (2,192 pass, 5 skipped as ever; the owner's own case among them: seven seeds, nine met, two thousand heard of, reads 7/2), `ruff`, and the app's tests (132). **Not seen:** the corrected numbers on a real torrent. That's the owner's next click on Count.

Looked at again every two seconds and whenever a film is joined or the setting changes; libtorrent is told only when the answer changes.

With the switch on, nothing more is sent once a movie is all here, and the speed is greyed; switched off, the speed last chosen is back. The note under them says what each does, and that a movie still arriving is sent on freely either way.

- The owner's setting was on None, so their switch comes up on, with Max as the speed to go back to.

2026-10-12, midday. **Full screen and back with a film open: a second a step, now 17 thousandths.** The owner: the app is extremely slow when a video is double-clicked to the whole screen, and again coming back to a window.

- **Measured first.** In the unseen copy with a test video playing in the film player, the window resized to the screen's size in twelve steps took **1.1 to 1.4 seconds a step** (thirteen to seventeen seconds in all); with no film open, 15 thousandths. A sample of the app during it showed the main thread waiting for one lock: macOS takes the film's drawing lock each time the film's place in the window changes, and the drawing thread had it nearly all the time.
- **Why it had it:** mpv hands a frame over a little before it's due, and waits out that time inside the call that draws it. The app held the drawing lock around that call, so through the wait too. The lock isn't taken in turn, so the drawing thread kept getting it back ahead of macOS.
- **The fix** (`FilmGLView`): the frame's moment is waited for with the lock let go, then the frame is drawn with the lock held only for the drawing, and mpv is told not to wait again. Nothing about when a frame is shown changes.
- **mpv's header is wrong about a unit** (the rule about outside libraries: check the installed one). `mpv_render_frame_info.target_time` is documented as millionths of a second on `mpv_get_time_us`'s clock; the mpv this app is built with (0.41) gives thousand-millionths on `mpv_get_time_ns`'s. Taken at the header's word, every frame waited its longest and four in five were dropped (seen in the first try, in mpv's own count). The code now reads it as whichever clock it's near, and waits for nothing if it's near neither.
- **Checked,** all in the unseen copy with the same test video: resizing with the film open, **16 to 17 thousandths of a second a step**; mpv's own count over the whole video, with the drawing as it was and as it is: no frame dropped and sound and picture 0.000 s apart, both times. The app's tests pass (133).
- **Not seen:** the owner's own double-click on a real film, on the real screen (the unseen copy can't take the screen). And this is the film player only: a channel's video or a song's video goes to the whole screen another way, which wasn't measured here.

2026-10-12, afternoon. **The bar across the top of a full-screen film is never shown.** The owner: going to full screen "it still has that long rectangle on the top of the screen for a few seconds... it disappears, but it shouldn't be there to begin with", and that they'd raised it three to five times.

- **What it is:** in full screen macOS keeps a window's top bar in a small window of its own, across the top of the screen. With the bar's buttons taken away for a film, that window is left as an empty strip. The mend of 7 October made it see-through once full screen had been reached.
- **Why it still showed** (measured on the real screen with a test copy and a new check, `MUSICORG_BENCH`'s `windows`, which writes out every change to the app's own windows): macOS makes the strip's window about half a second before full screen is reached, out of sight; shows it at the moment full screen is reached; and **a twentieth of a second after the app made it see-through, makes it solid again**. It then stayed until the app asked a second time, half a second later, or later still when the app was busy: before this morning's fix a film held the app up for seconds on the way to full screen, which is the "few seconds".
- **The fix** (`AppModel.tidyFullScreenTopBar`): what's in the strip's window is hidden, not the window made see-through. macOS leaves that as it's put, and the window has no colour of its own, so it draws nothing. And it's done as soon as macOS makes the window (looked for sixty times a second from the moment full screen begins), half a second before it's ever shown.
- **Only in the Warm Look**, which is the owner's: in the native look that window draws nothing in the first place, which is why checks in a test copy's fresh settings never saw the strip. A check of this is run with `-appLook warm`.
- **Checked** on the real screen, test copy in the Warm Look, the strip's own window pictured by itself twenty times a second: left alone, **67% of it is drawn** for as long as the film has the screen; with the fix, **0%** from the first picture (a hundredth of a second after it's shown) to the last, into full screen twice.
- **Not seen:** with the owner's own eyes in their own app; and a song's video or the visualizer given the whole screen, which goes the same way through the same code but wasn't run (the test copy has no song with a video).
- New checks for a developer: `MUSICORG_BENCH`'s `fullscreen` and `windows`, in `Bench`.

2026-10-12, afternoon. **Space, and the arrows, work a film.** The owner: "when playing a movie/tv show/video, space bar should pause/play, the arrow keys should lower/raise volume, and the side keys should skip forward and back."

- **Space did the wrong thing in a film:** it was taken by the music player's own space bar before the film's Pause button could have it, so it started or paused the music under the film. Now, while a film is open, it pauses and plays the film.
- **The arrows** (`PlayerKey` in the Kit, tested there): up and down move the sound a twentieth; left and right go ten seconds back and on, never before the start and never onto the very end. Held down, an arrow carries on; the space bar doesn't flip back and forth.
- **When they're the player's:** while a film is open (films, episodes, any video file: it has the whole window), and while a video or the visualizer has the whole screen. **Not** for a video playing inside the window: there the arrows still move through the lists and the sidebar, as they always have, and space plays and pauses as before. With ⌘, ⌥, ⌃ or Shift held a key is left alone (⌘→ is still Next Song), and so is typing in a field, a sheet and a file panel.
- A key that worked the player brings its controls up for three seconds, as moving the mouse does, so the sound's bar or the film's is seen to move.
- **Checked** in the unseen copy with a test film (`MUSICORG_BENCH`'s new `key=` and `film`): space paused it and played it again and the music stayed off; up raised the sound; right went on ten seconds, left back, and right pressed past the end stopped a second short of it; with no film open, down and up still moved through a song list. On the real screen, in full screen: space and right worked the film. **Not run:** the arrows on a song's video or the visualizer on the whole screen, and a real keyboard (the presses were made up by the app's test walker).
- My choices, the owner's to change: ten seconds each way (the buttons on screen are ten back and thirty on), and a twentieth of the sound a press.

2026-10-12, evening. **"Keep" is "Download"; a bigger Customise Home; channels' pictures that had gone.** Three of the owner's list of that evening.

- **Keep → Download.** On a film's ways to play the button is **Download** and **Stop Downloading**, and the lines about it say "Downloading: 34% here", "Downloaded to your Movies folder as…", "Couldn't download it". The engine's two messages that reach the screen changed with them. Only the words: the code, the RPC methods (`torrent.keep`, `torrent.stop_keeping`) and the contract still say keep, and nothing about what's fetched or where it goes changed.
- **Customise Home is bigger:** 720 points wide and as tall as the screen has room for, up to 980 (it was 560 by 640, and with twelve rows on the page only about eight of the rows that could be added were in sight).
- **Channels' pictures.** The owner: in Video Finder's channels, many of the pictures never appear. Measured: of the first 96 channels the channels add-on lists, **63 pictures' addresses answer "not found"**. The add-on's list ends in 2023, and a channel's picture has a new address whenever the channel changes it. Now a channel's card whose picture can't be had (gone, or never given) asks for the channel as it is today (`channel.videos` with `limit` 1: one request, through the one gate and its limiter, one channel at a time, the card on screen first) and the picture's address is remembered by the app on this Mac (`ChannelPictures` in the Kit: the newest two thousand), so each channel is asked about once. A channel's own page remembers its picture too, having read it anyway.
- **Checked:** in the unseen copy, with three made-up channels followed in a scratch library, a picture server on this computer and recorded answers: the channel whose picture had gone and the one given none both showed today's picture, the one whose picture was there kept it and wasn't asked about, and each address was asked for once. The count of 63 in 96 is from the real add-on's list.
- **Not tried:** against the real service (the unseen copy never is), so how long a page of sixty missing pictures takes to fill is reckoned, not seen: about a minute and a half at the limiter's pace, once. Downloads and searches share that pace while it does.

2026-10-12, evening. **Home: Continue Watching plays from where it was left, and rows for what hasn't been watched.** Two more of the owner's list.

- **Continue Watching.** A click on a movie or a series there opened its page; a channel's video already played. Now a movie or an episode **plays at once, from where it was left**, the way it was played before: the way to play (the add-on's stream: an address, or a torrent's hash, file and trackers) and the name it was played under are remembered with what was watched (`WatchHistory.Entry.stream`, `playName`; kept by the app on this Mac, as that list always was), and the film player opens a film where it was left already. One watched before this was remembered, or with nothing playable, opens its page as before; **Open Its Page** is in the card's right-click menu. Recently Watched still opens the page.
- **For a torrent this is the owner's own click on that film,** the same as Play on its page: the torrent is joined again for it. No promise about torrents is wider for it (CLAUDE.md says so now).
- **Rows for what hasn't been watched** (Customise Home; none is on the page until it's switched on): **Movies You Haven't Watched** and **Series You Haven't Watched** are the ones given a heart and not watched past a quarter (`WatchHistory.isBegun`, the owner's 25% mark; a quarter exactly isn't past it). **Videos You Haven't Watched** is the newest videos from the channels followed, without the ones watched past a quarter: a video has no heart of its own, so the channels followed stand in.
- **Checked** in the unseen copy, replaying recorded answers, with a half-watched test film and two hearted ones put in its own records: Home showed the half-watched one under Continue Watching with its bar at half, and only the other under Movies You Haven't Watched; a click on the Continue Watching card opened the film playing at 21 seconds of 40 (it was left at 20).
- **Not tried:** a real film from an add-on or a torrent (an add-on's address may have lapsed by the time it's clicked again: the player then says it couldn't be opened, and the page is a right-click away), a series' episode, and the videos row against real channels.

2026-10-12, evening. **Where the bar across a full-screen film still came from; the ✕ in the top left corner; the sound-track and subtitle menus say what's chosen.** The owner, with last night's mend in the build they were using: "the bar on full screen is back, it does disappear but its still there for a second. Tell me where it comes from?"

- **Where it came from: macOS's own picture of the window growing.** Last night's mend hid the strip itself (macOS's window for the top bar in full screen), and that held: measured again today, none of it was drawn at any moment. But while a window grows into full screen, macOS covers the screen with **a picture it makes itself, of where the window is going**, and shows that for the 0.85 seconds the growing takes. In the Warm Look it painted the title bar into that picture, as a band of the window's colour across the top of the film. A picture of the strip isn't the strip: nothing done to the real one could reach it, and it went when the picture did, which is the "second" the owner saw.
- **Measured** on the real screen (`MUSICORG_BENCH`'s `windows`, which now also says what's across the top of that picture, and saves small copies of it with `MUSICORG_WINDOW_SHOTS=<folder>`): the picture's top band was at brightness 15 where the film just under it was at 47; in the native look, 44 beside 46 (no band). Of the Warm Look's three window settings it was the see-through title bar that made macOS paint it.
- **The mend:** while a film, a video or the visualizer has the window, the title bar is macOS's plain one from the moment full screen begins (`AppModel.plainTitleBar`), which macOS leaves out of its picture; it's the look's again when full screen is left, or when the film is shut in full screen. After: 42 beside 47 as the picture fades in, 47 beside 49 when it's whole.
- **A video's or the visualizer's Full Screen button had the same band for another reason:** full screen began in the same instant as the button's click, before the picture was over the window and the top bar's buttons had gone, so macOS's picture was of the page with its top bar. Full screen now begins a tenth of a second after the click. Measured: 6 beside 0 before (a band over a black screen), 0 beside 0 after.
- **The ✕ is in the top left corner** (the owner: "the X appears in the top left, not the bottom left"): a round button, a little way down from the corner so that it's clear of the window's own three buttons in a window, and of the edge the menu bar comes down from in full screen. A film's Close has gone from the bottom left. A video or the visualizer on the whole screen has the same ✕ (it leaves full screen; Leave Full Screen is still at the bottom right), with the song's name beside it, **and the dark band that ran across the top behind that name is gone**: nothing runs across the top of a full screen now.
  - The ✕ lies under macOS's hidden strip in full screen, which is why a button up there didn't work on 2026-10-03. The strip now lets clicks through while it's hidden. Measured in full screen: a press on the ✕ lands on the app's window.
- **Sound Track and Subtitles** (the owner: "make sure if there are subtitles or different audio tracks, they appear to be selected. only if there are subtitles or audio tracks to pick from"): each menu is in the film's controls only when there's something to choose (two sound tracks or more; any subtitles), and its button now **says what's chosen** ("Sound: English", "Subtitles: Off") instead of being a bare icon. Play and pause stay in the very middle whatever is beside them.
- **Checked:** a test film with two sound tracks and two sets of subtitles showed both menus, saying "Sound: English" and "Subtitles: English"; a film with one sound track and no subtitles showed neither. Seven runs on the real screen in the Warm Look for the band (a film into full screen and out, shut and opened again there; a picture into full screen and out).
- **Not tried:** the menus opened by a hand (the unseen copy can't open a menu), and the pointer at the very top edge in full screen, where macOS brings down its own menu bar: that's macOS's, and the ✕ is placed clear of it.

2026-10-12, evening. **Back is a solid button; the arrow keys work everywhere.** Two more of the owner's list.

- **Back** (the owner: "you need to click exactly on the line, make it a solid button"): on a channel's page, a film's or a series', a found playlist's and an artist's, the way back was a bare arrow, and only the arrow's own thin strokes took the click. It's one button now on all five, `BackButton`: a rounded "‹ Back", every part of it a place to click, and it answers the click that brings the app forward too (it's harmless). Checked in the unseen copy: a click near its right-hand end, well off the arrow, went back to the list.
- **The arrows** (the owner: "Arrow keys function everywhere, on every video, song playing (skips, adjusts volume), on a homepage (allows for the user to switch through whats on the window)"). Until now they were the player's only while a film was open or a picture had the whole screen. Whose they are is reckoned in the Kit (`ArrowKeys.owner`), in this order:
  - A field being typed in, a sheet or a panel keeps its keys.
  - While the picture is what's looked at (a film open, a video or the visualizer on the whole screen, **or the player's own page showing**), all four are the player's: left and right go ten seconds back and on, up and down move the sound.
  - A list on the page that has the keyboard (a song list after a click on a row) keeps up and down, which move through its rows; left and right, which a list has no use for, go back and on in what's playing.
  - **On Home the arrows move a highlight from card to card:** left and right along a row and on to the row's next cards, up and down to the nearest row that has cards (scrolling to it), **Return opens the card** as a click does, and Esc takes the highlight off (`CardFocus` in the Kit, `CardKeys` in the app). No highlight is drawn until an arrow is pressed.
  - On any other page, **whenever a song or a video is in the player, the arrows are the player's.** With nothing playing they're left alone, and move through the sidebar as macOS has them.
- **A change to what was there:** with a song playing and the sidebar holding the keyboard, up and down used to step through the sidebar's rows; they move the sound now. Going through the sidebar by key still works when nothing is playing, and carries on past Home without being caught by its cards (right steps into them).
- ⌘← and ⌘→ are still Previous and Next Song; the arrows alone go ten seconds, as in a film.
- **Checked:** ten new tests of whose the arrows are and where the highlight goes. In the unseen copy, on a Home with made-up rows: down put the highlight on the first card there was, right twice took it to the third, down went to the row under, up came back, Esc took it off, and on Songs the arrows weren't Home's any more. A film's keys work as before (ten seconds on, the sound up two steps, space pauses).
- **Not tried:** the arrows with a song playing (the unseen copy never plays one: it would take the media keys from the app in use); that path is the film's own, with the music player in the film player's place. Return on a highlighted card, by hand.

2026-10-12, night. **Settings is one page in the owner's five groups; "plugins"; terms and a privacy policy; the next episode plays by itself, behind a switch.** The owner's answers to what was asked on the 12th: "settings: Profile first - Player, Plugins, Sync - Move lyrics here, General", "make it a settings switch. for autoplay.", and "You can add a basic terms and privacy policy that what this app is used for doesnt fall back to the developer, any irresponsible use is solely on the user." (UI language: "can wait for another day".)

- **Settings.** One page that scrolls, the groups named down the left: a click on a name goes to that part, and the name of the part in sight is the one marked. In the owner's order:
  - **Profile:** the profiles and their sign-ins, then Deleted Items.
  - **Player:** what was Play Options, with the new switch for the next episode at its top.
  - **Plugins:** what was Add-ons, then Downloads (where downloads go, the day's limit, quality, the Movies and Videos folders, conversion, upload speed).
  - **Sync:** what was Sync, then **Lyrics**, moved here at the owner's word.
  - **General:** App Layout (it was under Profile), Terms of Use, Privacy.
  - The app's own sentences that send the owner to a section say the new names ("Settings → Player", "Settings → Plugins → Downloads", "Settings → Profile → Deleted Items", "Settings → General"). A page that opens Settings at one part (Import Playlists → Spotify) still does.
  - **Measured** (the unseen copy, a debug build): opening Settings the first time keeps the app busy 0.55 s, once; going from group to group 0.03 to 0.28 s the first time each is made and less after; coming back to Settings from another page 0.1 s. Each part is made when it's scrolled to: with all five groups made at once the first opening took 1.03 s. Before, a section was made when its name was clicked (a quarter of a second each, the first time).
  - Deviation from `CLAUDE.md` as it stood ("the sections of Settings are kept … by the Settings page"): it's one page now, and CLAUDE.md says so.
- **"Plugins".** The owner's word for an add-on. The app's own sentences say plugin (Settings, the Finders' empty pages, a film's ways to play), and the engine's sentences are reworded as they arrive (`Wording.plugins`), as "YouTube" is. The engine, its API, `addons.json` and the protocol they follow keep the word add-on, and so do these docs when they mean the engine's side.
- **Terms of Use and Privacy** (`Legal` in the Kit; Settings → General; `docs/TERMS.md`, `docs/PRIVACY.md`, word for word the same, held together by a test). Five short parts each, in plain words. The terms: the app supplies no media itself; what's searched for, played, downloaded, kept and shared, and which plugins are added, is the user's to answer for; a plugin is someone else's; no warranty; using the app is accepting them. The privacy policy says what the app really does: the developer collects nothing; the library, records and sign-ins stay on the computer; what leaves it is what a request takes (the service or plugin asked sees what was asked for and the internet address; a torrent's other people see the address); sync is off until switched on and stays on the home network. **They're a plain-words draft, not a lawyer's work**, and the owner was told so: words like these set out who answers for what, but can't by themselves take the law's own answer away.
- **The next episode by itself** (Settings → Player → "Play the next episode when one ends", **off as standard**). When an episode of a series plays to its end the next one starts, by the owner's rule: **the same quality from the same plugin; failing that, the next plugin in their list that has that quality; failing that, the film is shut and the episode's page opens with its ways to play, to ask.** Which episode is next (the next in its season, then the next season's first; never a special, never one that isn't out yet) and which way is taken are reckoned in the Kit (`NextEpisode`); the film player says when a film has reached its end (`FilmPlayer.onEnded`); and an episode played again from Home's Continue Watching leads on in the same way (`WatchHistory.Entry.episodeId`, `source`).
  - **A change to a promise, made at the owner's word:** a torrent was joined only from the owner's click on it. With this switch on, the next episode's torrent is joined with no click. The switch is the owner's standing word for that, and its note in Settings says so in those words; CLAUDE.md records the exception and its limits. With the switch off nothing is joined that wasn't clicked, as before. The torrent of the episode that ended is left before the next is joined (unless it's the same torrent: a season in one).
- **Checked:** eight new tests of which episode is next and which way is taken, two of the terms. In the unseen copy with a made-up series of three nine-second episodes served from this computer by two made-up plugins: with the switch on, episode 1 played from the first plugin in 1080p was followed by episode 2 the same way, then episode 3 (which the first plugin had only in 720p) in 1080p, and nothing after the last; with neither plugin having episode 3 in 1080p, the film shut and the series' page showed "Where to Play S1 E3" with episode 3 picked; with the switch off, the film stayed on its last frame. Settings pictured at the top of each group, each name marked in its turn.
- **Not tried:** the next episode from a real plugin or a torrent (nothing here joins a real torrent but the owner's own click, or now their switch); the switch itself clicked by hand.

2026-10-12, night. **SoundCloud, the second place music comes from, with a converter.** The owner, to what was asked on the 12th: "Soundcloud yes, comes from youtube and soundcloud, provide a converter so all songs end up as the same format." Three standing rules change with it, each at their word; CLAUDE.md, the contract (section 3) and `ENGINE_API.md` record them.

- **Nothing new is installed.** The yt-dlp the engine already uses reads SoundCloud. Checked against the installed version (2026.09.27) with one live search and one track read: a search is `scsearchN:<words>`; a track is `https://api.soundcloud.com/tracks/<number>`; an entry has `id`, `title`, `uploader`, `duration`, `view_count`, `timestamp` and `thumbnails` (the 500-pixel square is `t500x500`). **The formats on offer differ from what was expected:** the track read had only 128 kbps MP3 (`http_mp3_1_0`, `hls_mp3_1_0`); the track downloaded for the check below was offered AAC too, at 96 kbps (`hls_aac_96k`). SoundCloud's "original file" needs a sign-in, and isn't taken.
- **The engine** (`youtube`, the one gate: rule 8 now says "and to SoundCloud"). A SoundCloud track's id is **`sc:` and its number**, taken wherever a video's id is:
  - `search.soundcloud` (new; `youtube.soundcloud_search`): tracks in the Candidate shape. The artist is who uploaded it; there's no album and no explicit mark, so a child's profile is given none.
  - `youtube.stream` gives a SoundCloud track's MP3 to play.
  - A `download` plan takes it: downloaded through the throttled queue like every download, counted in the day's limit, tagged `MUSICORG_SOURCE` `soundcloud` (a new enum value), with its own picture as its cover (it has no album) and lyrics looked for by name as for any song. It's a download in every list and rule that asks (`tags.DOWNLOAD_SOURCES`): Discover → Downloads, Delete, what's shared with a phone as a download.
  - YouTube Music is never asked about a SoundCloud track (`get_track`, `get_lyrics` answer None for one), and SoundCloud turning a request away is an ordinary failed download: it never pauses the queue as YouTube's refusal does.
- **The converter (rule 6's one transcode).** A SoundCloud download is left as **AAC in M4A, the library's one format**: SoundCloud's own AAC, where it has one, is repackaged and not re-encoded; an MP3 is converted to AAC at 160 kbps. yt-dlp does it with its own ffmpeg step (`FFmpegExtractAudio`, `m4a`) inside the job's `_Staging` folder, so no new writer exists (rule 3 is as it was). What doesn't come out as AAC in M4A goes to review (`soundcloud_not_converted`, a new review reason) and isn't kept. `MUSICORG_SOURCE_FORMAT` is what SoundCloud delivered, so a converted file says so. **Said plainly to the owner:** converting an MP3 can't add anything to it and loses a little, for good; it's the price of one format. Nothing else is converted: not a YouTube Music download, not a rip, not a file already in the library.
- **The app.** Music Finder's search has a choice beside the search box, **Music Service | SoundCloud** (`MusicSearchSource`, remembered; changed with a search on the page, the search is run again there). SoundCloud's results are the same rows: Play, Queue, Download, plays and length; no Artist button (who uploaded a track isn't an artist the Artist page would know). A SoundCloud download is listed under Discover → Downloads like any other (`Track.isDownload`).
- **Checked.** Fifteen new engine tests and two in the Kit; the whole engine suite (2,207) passed before the cover was added, and the tests round it after. **yt-dlp's own conversion was run for real on files made in the test, with no network:** an MP3 came out as AAC in M4A of the same length with nothing else left in the job's folder, and an AAC file came out with its sound unchanged (the decoded audio's hash is the same). **One real track** (Creative Commons, 9½ minutes) went through the real plan and queue into a scratch library: one file under its uploader's name, tagged `soundcloud`, `sc:<number>`, `hls_aac_96k`, 96 kbps AAC, nothing left in staging. In the unseen copy, replaying the recorded search: the choice beside the box, and five results listed with Queue and Download.
- **Not tried:** a SoundCloud track played in the app (the unseen copy never plays one), a track that's MP3-only downloaded from SoundCloud itself (the conversion was proven on a local MP3), and a download clicked in the app.
- **For the owner to know, or decide:** (1) some tracks arrive as 96 kbps AAC, SoundCloud's own, kept as it came: under the 100 kbps a YouTube Music download must reach, and taken ahead of the 128 kbps MP3 because it isn't converted. Say if the MP3, converted, should be taken first instead. (2) SoundCloud's terms allow downloading only where the uploader switched it on; this takes any track's stream, which the owner chose knowing it (asked on the 12th). (3) SoundCloud tracks are named as their uploaders named them ("… | Free Download"); Edit Details mends one. (4) Discover's picks, Covers & Remixes and imports still look only at the music service.

2026-10-12, late. **Export and Import, under Settings → Profile.** The owner, to what was asked on the 12th ("It lets the engine write a copy of the library outside the library, which is a rule change: yes?"): "yes allow for a rule change." CLAUDE.md (rule 2's exceptions) and the contract (section 1) record it.

- **An export** is one new folder, in a place the owner chooses (a drive, a folder), holding what they tick: **Music** (the library's songs, with their covers and lyrics, as they lie), **Videos** (saved music videos, and the videos kept outside the library), **Movies and series** (the kept films, with their `Series` and `Anime` folders), **Playlists, favourites and listening** (the library's `state.json`), and **Settings and plugins**. `export.json` in it says what's inside, and is written last: a folder without it is an export that didn't finish.
- **No sign-in is ever in an export.** Not `accounts.json`, not the paired devices. A plugin set up the owner's own way has that set-up in its address, which can hold a key: its address is left out, and it's named (in the page before exporting, and in `export.json`) as one to add again by hand. Of the app's own settings only a named list travels (`SettingsCarrier` in the Kit): never the switch that shares the library with devices, never the profiles' folders; and what's been watched goes **without the way each film was played** (an address a plugin's set-up may have written a key into). A test writes a made-up key in each of those places and looks for it in every file of the export.
- **Where it may be written** (`fileops.make_export_folder`, `fileops.export_file`, the only writers of it): always a new folder (` (2)` if the name is taken), never inside the library or a source folder, never overwriting, each file whole or not there (a temp file beside it, renamed). The library is only read. Not journaled: no library file changes.
- **An import** brings an export into the profile in use. The export is a source: only read. **Songs** come in by `copy_in`, in one batch of a new kind, `import`, that undo takes back; a song the library has already (the same `MUSICORG_ID`) is left out, so importing twice adds nothing. A cover or lyrics file comes only where nothing of its name is there. **Films and videos** go where kept ones go (`keep_media`, from the export folder); one of the same name and size is left. **Records are taken only by a library that has none of its own**: nobody's playlists, hearts or plays are written over, and the page says to import into a new profile to have a profile exactly as it was. **Plugins** the owner doesn't have are added to the end of their list. Before anything is written the page says what would come (`dry_run`).
- **The engine:** module `transfer`; `profile.contents`, `profile.export`, `profile.import`, `profile.transfer`, and the notification `profile.transferred`. An export or import runs on a thread of its own, one at a time, and says how far it has got (a few thousand songs are minutes of copying).
- **The app:** Settings → Profile → **Export and Import**: the five tick-boxes with what each amounts to ("1,013 files · 4.2 GB"), Export… (asks where), Choose an Export… (says what it would bring, then Import Into "<profile>"), a bar while either runs, and how it ended (Show in Finder for an export).
- **Checked:** eight engine tests (what an export holds; no key anywhere in one; never inside the library; `export_file` keeping to its folder, never writing over, and leaving nothing when a copy fails; a whole export brought into a second library with its songs indexed, its records, its films in their folders, a second import adding nothing, and undo taking the songs back out; a library with records keeping its own; only a whole export of this format read; one at a time), one over RPC, four in the Kit. The whole engine suite, 2,216, passed. The page pictured in the unseen copy with a scratch library: the counts, and the made-up plugin with its own address named as left out.
- **Not tried:** an export or import clicked in the app (the folder chooser can't be worked unseen), a library of real size (the time it takes is reckoned, not measured), and a drive that fills up part way (the copy that fails leaves no half file, tested; the export folder is left without its `export.json`, and the page says why).
- **Left for later, and said to the owner:** an import can't yet make the new profile for you (make it first, then import); the engine's own settings (the day's limit, the folders chosen for films) don't travel; and undo takes back the songs an import brought, not the films or the records.

2026-10-13, evening. **A song that wouldn't play on a busy Mac; a search of everything; a green check on what's been watched; Terms and Privacy behind buttons.** The owner's four notes after a day with last night's build.

- **"…can't be played from the service: … The deno at … can't be used: it didn't answer within 20 seconds. Install it with `brew install deno`…"** (the owner's picture, reported as a fault playing from SoundCloud). The song's id in it is a YouTube one, and nothing was wrong with deno: **the Mac was too busy to start it.** Measured while looking: the load average was 96 (later over 300: another project's Xcode build and an iPhone simulator), and `deno --version`, a fifth of a second on a quiet Mac, took 10.6 seconds. Three faults of the engine's made that into a song that wouldn't play:
  - **Every look-up at YouTube ran `deno --version` first**, and every download `ffmpeg -version` too, each allowed twenty seconds. A copy found usable is now remembered for as long as the engine runs and its file is unchanged (`tools.require`); one that can't be used isn't remembered, and is tried again next time.
  - **SoundCloud was made to wait on deno, which it doesn't need** (it's YouTube's helper). A SoundCloud search, play or download no longer asks for it at all.
  - **The message was wrong twice over.** It told the owner to install a program that's installed; and because the install hint has `curl -fsSL` in it, the error sorter read "ssl" and called it "failed on the network". A helper that's too slow to start now says so: "This computer is very busy just now … Try again in a moment", with nothing about installing; a helper that's missing is said as itself (it's worked out before the request joins the limiter's line, so it's never taken for YouTube turning a request away).
  - **Checked for real:** a SoundCloud track looked up for playing gave an https address that answered with `audio/mpeg`, partial content, an MP3's first bytes: what the app's player needs. Still not heard by anyone: say if a SoundCloud song won't play now.
- **A search looks through everything** (the owner: the search "needs to expand … it searches the whole database … it needs to be able to bring in all the avengers"). Movie Finder, Series Finder and Anime Finder searched only the list that was chosen, and a list that takes no search (New, Best Rated) had no search box at all. Now the box is always there, wider, and says "Search all movies"; a search asks **every plugin** (`addon.search`, new; module `addons`), whichever list or genre is showing, and puts what they find together, each title once, in the owner's order of plugins.

So each plugin is also asked once more with "the" before the words, and of that answer only titles with every word of the search in their name are added. **For real, with the app's own four plugins: 23 movies for "avengers" where there were 16**, and 10 series. A plugin decides how many it gives: with the owner's other plugins asked too, there are more. The Finder kept apart searches its one list, as before.

It can move when their drawing of the sidebar's Watched arrives.

2026-10-14, before dawn. **A series from one torrent: the picture that broke up while an episode arrived, and "play next" that played the same episode again. Subtitles on the picture. The Watchlist.** The owner's notes after an evening of watching, and a second message while the work was under way.

- **The picture broke into pink and purple while an episode was arriving, and played cleanly once it had all arrived** (the owner: "I think it's an issue when the video is nearing download completion"). **Found, and measured:** the engine waited for libtorrent to say it had a piece (`have_piece`), then read that piece's bytes from the film's file. A piece libtorrent has has passed its check, but its last blocks may still be waiting to be written: read in that moment, the file holds whatever was there before, and the film player was handed that as film. With an open-licence film (Blender's Sintel) played from its torrent through the engine's own player in a scratch cache, arriving no faster than it was read (as an episode does), **one part in 121 that was served wasn't what the finished file holds.** A film that arrived much faster than it was read showed nothing wrong (0 in 495), which is why it had passed before.
  - **The mend:** a film's bytes are asked of libtorrent itself, a piece at a time (`read_piece`, answered in an alert; the engine's session now listens for those and nothing else), and never read from the file while it arrives. The pieces just read are kept in memory (48 MB a torrent at most), so each is asked for once. **After: 0 in 121, twice.**
  - **The same fault could have put holes in a film being downloaded** (it was copied to the Movies folder the moment its last piece arrived). It's copied now only once libtorrent says the film is all on the disk (`flush_cache`).
  - A read never runs past the end of its own file into the next one's bytes (found by the new tests; what the address gave was already cut to length).
- **"Play next" restarted the episode that had just ended, under the next one's name** (the owner: "it restarts the current film, but says its showing the next episode"; and the next one, opened by hand afterwards, "keeps the progress of the previous … and doesn't start"). A season that's one torrent has an episode in each file; asked for the next file of a torrent it had already joined, the engine gave back the address of the file it was joined for. So the same episode played again, the app remembered its place under the next episode's name, and the next episode itself was never fetched.
  - **The mend:** each file of a torrent has its own address (`…/<torrent>/<file's number>`), and a torrent asked for another of its files turns to it (`_Joined.turn_to`): that file is fetched from then on. One that's being kept keeps fetching its own film, and the other is fetched beside it. A player part way through one file keeps that file, whatever the torrent turns to. Checked for real with the same torrent: another file's address gave that file's own bytes, and going back gave the film's.
  - **The next episode, started by itself, starts at its beginning** (`FilmPlayer.open`'s `fromStart`), not where it was left another time.
  - The app asked a torrent "how is it going" in two loops at once after a next episode from the same torrent; now one.
  - **What the fault left behind:** an episode that was "played next" in the last two days may have a wrong place remembered, and open part way through once. Dragging the bar back mends it for good. (Nothing was cleared automatically: the places are the owner's records, and the wrong ones can't be told from the right.)
- **Subtitles** (the owner: they "should be right under the video frame, not at the bottom of the page", in a window; full screen was fine). In a window taller than the film, mpv put them in the black band under the picture: at the very bottom of the window, under the controls. They're drawn on the picture now, along its bottom edge, the same in a window as in full screen (`sub-use-margins` off). Pictured before and after on the real screen with a test film in a square window.
  - Seen in the same picture: in a narrow window, Play was drawn over the sound-track menu (the two had been laid over each other since the 12th). They're side by side now, and what gives way in a narrow window is the empty room, not the buttons.
- **The Watchlist** (the owner: "watch list isnt there", then "watchlist should be on each movie, and series page too, say you click on a tv show, you can add it to watchlist"). The Watched list was there, but in a menu (Movie Finder's and Series Finder's list of lists), where it wasn't found; and what was wanted is a list to add to.
  - **Add to Watchlist** on every movie's and series' page, under its name (On Your Watchlist once it's on; a click takes it off). Not in the Finder kept apart, where nothing is remembered.
  - **Watchlist, a row in the sidebar** under Videos, after Movies: **To Watch** (what was added, movies and series apart, the newest first; a click opens one's page, a right-click takes it off) and beside it **Watched** (what's been played, with the same green checks). Added without a drawing because the list couldn't be found where it was; it can be taken out with Customise Sidebar like any row.
  - The Finders' list menus have Watchlist beside Watched too. It's kept by the app on this Mac (`Watchlist` in the Kit), and travels with an export's records.
- **For checking:** `MUSICORG_BENCH` has `popups` (every pop-up menu's items read out, since a menu can't be opened in the unseen copy: it showed Watched had been in the menu all along) and `shot` (a picture of the window as the screen has it, a film's picture and subtitles included).
- **Checked:** nine new engine tests with a stand-in for libtorrent whose pieces are never on the disk (bytes come as libtorrent holds them; each piece asked for once; a piece that can't be handed over ends the read; the next file of a torrent; one played beside one kept; a part-way player keeps its file; each file's own address over HTTP); the real torrent check above, before and after; a Kit test of the Watchlist. In the unseen copy: Add to Watchlist clicked on a made-up film's page put it on the list, the sidebar's Watchlist page showed it, and the next-episode run with the made-up series still goes 1, 2, 3.
- **Not tried:** a real season in one torrent played in the app from one episode into the next (the engine's half is proven with a real torrent's files; the app's half with a made-up series of plain addresses), and subtitles written in the ASS style, which place themselves.

2026-10-15, late evening. **Watched by the owner's rule, Next Episode, the player that goes back when a film ends, Recommended series and anime, and the calendar's first version.** The owner's notes, with their drawing of the calendar.

- **Watched means nine tenths** (the owner: "only mark it in the sidebar as watched if the latest episode has been watched, or movie from start to 90%. this 10% accounts for credits"). The green check on a card, the Watchlist's Watched and the Finders' Watched lists all go by it. What the history already said had been played to the mark was taken in once.

The Watchlist reads its series' episodes again when they're six hours old. Anime has its own heading. A movie can be marked watched from its card's menu.

It plays it by the owner's rule for the next episode (the same quality from the same plugin, and so on), and as a click it doesn't wait on Settings → Player's switch. **The ✕ and Next Episode are solid buttons** that light up under the pointer and press in (the owner: "Give X and that button a solid pressable button"); the ✕ of a video or the visualizer on the whole screen is the same.

With the switch on, the next episode plays as before, and the player shuts after the last episode out. Only at the film's real end (its last minute or so, never more than its last tenth): a stream that stops part way is left where it stopped. Found while checking: a short test clip whose stream couldn't be read said "end" at its first second, and the first version of the rule took that for its end.
- **A film's page opened over another kept the other's trailer** (the owner: "it registered the view trailer from the page before it opened the show"). A movie's or series' page opened from Home or the Watchlist, while another was open in the same Finder, was the same page given a new film: until its own details came it showed the last one's trailer, episode and ways to play, and Play there could have played the other film under this one's name. Each film's page is a page of its own now. Found by reading the code; not seen happening.

Recommended Series, Recommended Movies and, in a new Anime group of rows, **Recommended Anime**: the popular lists of the three genres met most in what's on the Watchlist, watched and hearted, put together closest to that taste first, without anything already seen, listed or hearted (`Recommending`). Before, Recommended was the popular list of one leading genre, with what had been watched still in it. Recommended Series and Recommended Anime were put on the owner's Home once, after their series rows. Also offered in Customise Home: Popular Anime Now.

On each day: the episodes of the owner's series that come out then (hearted, on the Watchlist or watched), on this Mac's own day, and the videos the first eight followed channels put up (asked for on 2026-10-12: "shows when the next episode comes out, or ... your favourite channel uploads"). As many as fit, then "+2 more" with the rest in a small window. A click opens an episode on its series' page with its ways to play showing, or plays a video. Its colours are the look's, not the drawing's purple. The sidebar row was added without a drawing, like the Watchlist's; it can be taken out in Customise Sidebar.

2026-10-16, afternoon. **Import from Stremio** (the owner: "yes build the import from stremio. For Account D. Later we will do the same for Account C"), after their Stremio export turned out to hold one show copied 590 times.

- **Settings → Profile → Export and Import → From Stremio.** Read Stremio shows what the Stremio app on this Mac remembers; Import Into "<profile>" adds it to the profile in use. Stremio's storage (its web page's local storage in WebKit's folder) is copied into a folder of the app's own, read, and the copy deleted; only its Library's two records are read, never its sign-in. Stremio itself is never changed.
- **What's taken:** its Library onto the Watchlist; every episode it has as watched (Stremio keeps a bit for each episode, squeezed with zlib; lined up by the last one watched, in Cinemeta's season-and-number order, specials first); a movie it marks watched (it has its own mark, and counts one played most of the way), or one left past nine tenths; and every title watched into the history, with the episode and the minute it was left at, so Continue Watching has it. Each title's details are asked of the plugins (six at a time) for its episodes, genres and picture. Only titles from Cinemeta and Kitsu: another plugin's own ids and channels are left out, and nothing from a plugin for adults is ever kept here.
- **It adds and takes nothing away.** Titles already on the Watchlist stay where they are; what's already watched stays watched; a title watched here more lately than in Stremio keeps where it was got to here.
- **A film opened for the first time by a way to play starts where it was left another way** (`FilmPlayer.open`'s `otherwiseAt`, from the history): an import brings where, not how.
- The history keeps up to 1,000 titles (it was 200).
- **Found while checking:** the first run lined up 571 watched episodes where Stremio had 1,669: the engine lists a series' episodes newest first, and Stremio counts them in the show's own order. Put in that order, 1,667 line up; the other two are one series the plugins couldn't give episodes for that moment (the import says so, and importing again adds them).
- The privacy policy says so (docs/PRIVACY.md and `Legal`).
- **Checked:** four new Kit tests (the watched-episode record read and lined up past specials added since; Stremio's records and its export read, each title once; an import's plan, left-out titles, watched movies by either mark, where each was left; adding to the history without taking away), 195 in all. With the Stremio app's real storage, into a scratch profile in the unseen copy: 307 on the Watchlist, 1,667 episodes of 175 series and 138 movies watched, 564 titles in the history, 165 to carry on with, in 20 seconds (three minutes the first time, while the plugins' answers weren't cached); the Watchlist and the calendar pictured with them.
- **Not done:** the import into D itself: the owner's own click (it shows what it found first).

2026-10-16, evening. **Home sync with a video app on a phone or tablet: what's watched, both ways, and films** (the owner: "allow [it] to Sync with musicorganizer app Sync. This sync only brings in watchlist, watched, progress of the shows. and anything downloaded. Movies downloaded on the app also get moved over. It adds information, doesnt remove it ... A series watched on the tablet will show it watched on the C account on music organizer"). Asked, they chose: the device lists the Mac's films and they pick which come over; a film downloaded on the device is copied to the Mac and the device keeps its own. The video app is a separate project; the agreement is docs/ENGINE_API.md section 3, "What's watched, and films, for a video app".

- **Engine (`sharing`):** `GET`/`POST /sync/v1/watching` (a device's profile's watching, and the device's own, checked and cut to the agreement's limits and kept for the app in `watching.json`), `GET /sync/v1/films` (the Movies folder's films, fetched with the files request), `POST /sync/v1/uploads` and `PUT /sync/v1/uploads/<id>` (a device's film in parts, carried on from where it stopped, kept in the Movies folder by `fileops.keep_media` under `torrent.keep`'s names, never over a file). These answer a device of **any** profile, whichever profile the app has open; the music's requests still answer only the open profile's. `hello` says `watching` and `films` in `accepts`, and `known`. New Sync errors `not_shared` (403) and `wrong_place` (409). RPC `watching.set`, `watching.sent`, `watching.done`, notification `watching.changed`. New `fileops.write_upload_part` and `write_upload_note`, which write only inside the app's cache's `uploads/`.
- **App:** gives the engine each profile's watching (the open one's whenever it changes, every profile's when sharing starts), and adds what a device sent to the records of the profile it was paired with: the open profile's, or another's where it's put away (`WatchSync` in the Kit). Adds and never takes away: a title not on the Watchlist goes on it, an id not watched is watched, and of two places for one title the later wins. **What the owner takes off a Watchlist, or marks Not Watched, on one side stays so on that side** (`Watchlist.takenOff`, `Finished.unmarked`): otherwise the other side would bring it straight back; the other side keeps its own. A place sent without its title keeps one.
- Settings → Sync and the privacy policy say what a video app exchanges, and that a device belongs to the profile open when it's paired.
- **Checked:** 25 new engine tests (2,253 passed in all; ruff clean); 5 new Kit tests (the owner's example: the computer at episode 2 minute 5, the tablet on to episode 5 with 2 to 4 watched there, end up the same on both). In an unseen copy of the app with scratch folders and two test devices on this Mac (one of the open profile, one of another): both known; D's device's episodes 2 to 4 and its place at episode 5 reached D's records and came back to it; C's device's watching went into C's put-away records while D was open, and C's device saw none of D's; C's device was refused the music list, listed the films and fetched one in part (206); a film sent from D's device in two parts was kept byte for byte in the scratch Movies folder, a part sent from the wrong place was refused (409), and the same film again was `already`.
- **Found while checking:** a place a device sent without its title was kept with none, and so wasn't sent on (the Mac sends only places with a title to open). It now takes the title it knows, or one made from the place.

2026-10-16, night. **A profile showed the last profile's Watchlist and what it had watched** (the owner: "I did a streamio import, on D. But its showing all the watchlists and watched/not watched on C. They should be separate"). The records were kept apart on the Mac all along (C's own two titles, D's 307), but the app read the Watchlist, what's watched, the history, the favourites, each series' latest episode, Home's rows and the channels' pictures only when it opened, and kept them in memory across a switch of profile: C showed D's, and anything C saved (a film watched, the Watchlist page reading its series) would have written D's over C's own. Nothing had been written over: checked in the owner's own records. A switch now reads the next profile's own afresh. Checked in an unseen copy with two scratch profiles, switched back and forth: each shows only its own, and each keeps only its own. New Bench steps `profile=<id>` and `records`.

2026-10-17, evening. **The name Bear Media, the bear, a loading screen, a speed audit, and an engine that's always gone twenty seconds after the app.** The owner's notes, with their logo (a bear holding a ticket with a play button, on a night sky).

- **The name** (the owner: "use that image as the logo. And rename the app to Bear Media"): Bear Media under the icon, in the menu bar, the window and every sentence the app and the engine show, the terms of use and the privacy policy included (dated 17 October). What doesn't change, so nothing is lost: the app's id (`org.musicorganizer.app`, which its settings and records go by), the engine's folders and files, the library, its tags, the signing certificate's name, the repository and the code. `scripts/build_app.sh` builds `app/build/Bear Media.app` and takes the old `Music Organizer.app` away once it isn't open.
- **The bear** (`BearDrawing` in the app): drawn in code from the owner's 256-point picture, its colours and shapes measured off it, so it's sharp on the whole screen. It breathes, sways a little, blinks (once a double), flicks an ear, a sheen passes over its ticket, and the play button turns like a coin spun on a table. **The icon** is drawn from the same code (`scripts/make_icon.sh` → `app/Resources/AppIcon.icns`, in a macOS rounded square).
- **The loading screen** (the owner: "the loading screen should load whatever is needed for the app to immediately start. Minimum 5 seconds, it holds it until it's done loading"): the bear on its sky fills the window from the app's start until the library is read and the first page made under it, and at least five seconds; then it fades and the page is there. The window's toolbar is hidden under it. If getting ready takes over fifteen seconds, a line under the bear says macOS may be asking about the library's folder. A timing run (`MUSICORG_BENCH`) doesn't wait the five seconds. After a switch of profile the page that says "Reading your library…" is as before.
- **The engine took nearly five minutes to stop** after the app had quit (17:59:56 to 18:04:38 in the owner's log), so the app opened again found the library taken ("Another Music Organizer process … is using this library"). Whatever is left of stopping, the engine is now gone twenty seconds after it began (`rpc.SHUTDOWN_LIMIT_S`; the lock is the system's and goes with it; the journal and the index survive a stop at any moment). And the app opening finds a library still taken by an engine that's stopping waits for it, behind the bear, up to thirty seconds, before calling it a problem. Which part held on for those minutes isn't known (the log says only that sharing had stopped); the film player's part is being measured on its own.
- **The speed audit** (the owner: "Going through the watchlist is very slow. And in general the app slowed down quite a lot"). Measured in an unseen copy holding a copy of the owner's own records (187 on the Watchlist, about 2,000 watched, 1,000 in the history; sharing off in the copy), with a made-up plugin on this Mac answering every series after a quarter of a second, as one on the internet does:
  - **What's watched is worked out once** when the records change (`AppModel.watchedIds`, `newEpisodeIds`), not by every card on every page at every redraw. Before, each series whose episodes came in made every card on every page, those kept out of sight too, work it out again.
  - **Series read for the Watchlist are noted every two seconds, together, and saved once,** not one by one (each was a save of the records and a redraw of every page). And they're read again only when six hours old: every opening of the app read all of them (334 questions to the plugins in the copy), because the episodes read are kept only while the app is open. The calendar, which needs the episodes themselves, still reads those it hasn't got.
  - The Watchlist's grids are made as they're scrolled to (it was laid out whole, nearly 18,000 points tall).
  - The calendar puts every episode on its day away from the window's own work.
  - The watching given to the engine every ten seconds for a paired device is put together only when the records look different (it was the whole history and everything watched each time).
  - Before → after, the same steps: opening the Watchlist while its series were read, the window busy 4.6 s → 1.7–2.0 s of the 30; a screenful scrolled 0.8 s → 0.2–0.3 s; opening the calendar 1.3 s → 0.2 s. The app's own start was the same, about 0.8 s busy, in both (with the bear).
  - Quitting the copy took 0.05 s, so the owner's twenty seconds wasn't the app's own quitting; most likely the app was too busy to answer, as above.
- **In full screen the pointer goes with the controls** after five seconds of a still mouse, and comes back at the slightest move (the owner: "The mouse should disappear with all the other things on the screen after 5 seconds when using full screen"): a film in full screen (not paused, nothing wrong), and a video or the visualizer given the whole screen. The controls now stay five seconds, not three.
- **Checked:** a new engine test (a part of stopping made to hang; the engine leaves on time and the lock with it); the app's 200 tests; the whole engine suite; the timings above; the bear and the icon pictured (the loading screen in the unseen copy's window, then the songs under it).
- **Not seen:** the owner's app itself with the new name and icon, macOS's own full screen with the pointer hidden (an unseen copy can't take the screen).
2026-10-17, evening. **The Finders' genres and search, and a film or video to the Trash** (the owner's four reports).

- **Choosing a genre left the old "and …" in the second menu** ("the second one should always be empty, it keeps giving a *And Action* or whatever was previously selected there"). Nothing emptied it: not a new first genre, and not another list (which emptied only the first). Now the second is empty whenever the first changes, and both when another list is chosen; the same list chosen again keeps both (`GenreChoice` in the Kit).
- **"Searching removes any of the genre choosings."** A search never touched the genres, but a list being asked for turned every other asking away: a search cleared before its answer came (every plugin is asked, page after page) left that answer on the page under an empty box, the genres still showing above it and not applied. Shown in an unseen copy with HEAD's list and a slow made-up plugin. Now the newest asking wins and an older answer is left unshown (`Askings` in the Kit, `MediaList`). A genre chosen while a search of everything is showing puts the search away and shows that genre's list; in the Finder kept apart a search is of the one list and stays.
- **A click on a Finder's row in the sidebar puts its search away** ("clicking Series Finder after a search should move it back to series finder"), whether the page was showing or came back from another page: the box empties, a film open over the lists closes, and the lists come back at the genres chosen before (`AppModel.clickedInSidebar`, one counter for each row so other pages do no work). Movie, Series, Anime, the Finder kept apart, and Video Finder (its search and an open channel; a search answered after it was put away is left unshown). Home's View More does the same. Music Finder keeps its search, as before.
- **Typing in a Finder's search box lagged.** The search was only ever sent on Return, but the words were kept by the page, so each letter made the page work itself out again with its grid of cards. The box keeps them itself now (`FinderSearchField`). Unseen copy, debug build, a made-up plugin, eight letters typed 150 ms apart, twice each in turn: before 150–220 ms of the main thread held 10 ms or more (about 20 ms a letter); after, no letter held it 10 ms (0 ms, apart from 44–52 ms the first time the box takes the keyboard).
- **A film or video can be moved to the Trash** (at the owner's word: "should be able to right click and delete source material"). 2026-10-17. **Films from torrents: the next episode that waited for nearly all of itself, a film's start asked for first, "here: 5%" for a film that was all here, the pause at 100%, and stopping.** The owner's notes after a season played from one torrent: "when pressing next episode, it won't actually start playing until almost all the file is there, currently showed 98% here before it started playing. 99% here and it pauses the show when moving to 100%"; "is there any way to make sure that it loads the start of the film/series first?"; a film played to its end and opened again from the cache "still says 'here: 5%' ... it goes for example from 5,7,10,20,100"; and the app took about twenty seconds to quit, once reopening into "Another Music Organizer process ... is using this library".

- **The next episode waited for nearly all of itself.** Found: libtorrent applies a torrent's new file priorities a moment after it's asked to, and then gives every piece its file's priority again. The engine asked for the next episode's first pieces first (a deadline, which in libtorrent is the top priority) in that moment, so they became ordinary pieces of the episode, fetched rarest first, and a film's first pieces are among the commonest; and the player's first question then waited for its piece for up to two minutes without asking again. Measured: the top priority was taken away 20 times in 20 when the deadline was set at once, never when the priorities were let take hold first. With Blender's Sintel turned from one of its files to another and back, slowed to 1.5 MB/s, a place not yet here was answered after 83.6, 51.4 and 11.2 s, by when 100%, 70% and 21% of the film had arrived (the owner's 98%); now after 3 to 16 s (six tries), with 16 to 30% here.
  - **The mend** (`_Joined._choose`): the episode before's deadlines are cleared, nothing is asked for first until the new priorities hold, a range still waiting for its piece asks again every two seconds, and a player still reading the episode before is given what's here of it but nothing more is fetched for it.
- **A film's start and end first, then roughly in order from where it's played.** Opened, a film's first and last megabyte are asked for first (a player reads both before it shows anything: an MP4's index is often at its end, an MKV's cues are). As it plays, the megabyte just ahead of where it's read, then the next 32 MB and the 96 MB after that sooner than the rest (`_ahead`). Tried and dropped: libtorrent's own "in order" mode (it passes over what's asked for first whenever a rarer piece is waiting: Sintel's last 2 MB took 22 s, and the player waited 10 s half a megabyte in) and the rest of the film at libtorrent's lowest priority (no quicker). A film opened afresh is as quick as the people sharing it allow, as before: first bytes 2.5 to 4.7 s after the torrent's details in five tries (0.8 to 31 s before, in nineteen).
- **"here: 5%" for a film that was all here.** A torrent joined again was looked through by libtorrent from its start, every piece checked, before anything of it was known to be here (and its details fetched from the others again first). Now what libtorrent knows of a film is saved beside it in the cache when it's left (`torrents/.<hash>.resume`, through a new writer, `fileops.write_cached`, and only once the film's last parts are on the disk), and read back when it's played again: Sintel, all here, said 100% within a fifth of a second of being joined again, in the same engine and in a new one (it took 2.7 to 3.8 s before, climbing from 0%, and a 2 GB film takes many times that). It's used only while the film's files are still in the cache, and goes with them after their day. While libtorrent does check, `torrent.status` says `checking` (a new Torrent state) and the player says "Checking what's here…", not a low "here".
- **The pause at 100%.** The moment a film has all arrived, libtorrent writes out everything it still holds in memory before it reads anything more of the film, and it held up to 100 MB. With Sintel arriving as fast as it came and read at 2 MB/s across that moment, the longest wait then was 1.6 and 2.5 s before; now 0.04 and 0.12 s, with at most 16 MB held (`DISK_QUEUE_BYTES`; films still arrived at 7.5 to 12.3 MB/s with it, against 9.5 to 12.9 before). The player is also further ahead of where it's playing by then (26 to 33 MB in, against 11 to 18), so a wait has more to play through.
- **Stopping.** Measured with Sintel fetching at 4 to 13 MB/s: `Player.stop` took 2.8 to 3.5 s, then libtorrent ended on a thread of its own and held all of Python still for 1.3 to 1.9 s more, about 4.1 to 5.4 s in all. Now 3.1 to 4.7 s in all, ended inside `Player.stop` and logged ("stopped in … s"): the one-second sleep is gone, the film player's address stops listening within a tenth of a second, everyone else is let go of before what's here is noted, and trackers are given one second to hear this computer has gone (libtorrent's own was five). What's left is libtorrent closing its uTP connections (1.5 to 2.8 s, with Python held still): a session without uTP ended in 0 to 1 s, but uTP is how many people share, so that wasn't switched off. Closing one film (`torrent.stop`) returns at once; its leaving, and the noting, carry on by themselves.
- **Checked:** 17 new engine tests (the file priorities let take hold; the next episode from its start and the one before left alone; a waiting range asking again; the parts after it sooner, moved on as it's read; the checking state; a film's state saved beside it when closed, not saved when the disk is slow, saved for every film on stopping, used only while its files are there, and a film played again while being left waiting for that; and `write_cached`: replaced whole, a failed rename leaving the old note whole, a crash before the rename leaving nothing, refused outside the cache, in a folder that's a link, over a link or a folder, and under a junk name), 2,270 passed in all (5 skipped), ruff clean; a Kit test for the checking line. **The Sintel check** (scratch cache, slowed to 1.5 MB/s, read in 256 KB parts as a player does): first bytes 3.1 s after the torrent's details came (18 pieces here), the last 2 MB answered 13 s after they were asked for, 502 parts served and 0 different from the finished file, the longest wait within 20 s of it all arriving 0.11 s, and joined again 100% at once.

An answer with an HTTP code means the site was reached, so now the code decides the words: 410 or 451, "won't show its pages to this connection (HTTP 410). A site answers this way when it's closed to visitors from where the connection comes from"; 404 still "doesn't have that video any more"; 429 "too many requests"; 5xx "having trouble just now"; any other "refused that page (HTTP n)"; and only a connection that really failed (a time-out, a refused connection) "couldn't be reached". None of these says more of the address than the site's name. **Checked:** a new test of the wordings (17 page tests). Nothing gets round such a site: the engine asks with this Mac's own connection, so a VPN that covers the whole Mac covers Bear Media too.
