2026-10-18. **Open in Browser: Bear Media's own browser window, which finds the video a page plays and saves it** (the owner: "isnt there a way that, if the link hits this error. It offers a open in browser option, and then if you click yes, it pulls the info needed once it opens?", then "Build it, and build it so that even if the player is hiding the stream, it searches as many places as possible for it. Do a backup before this update, so that it can be reverted at any time."). Two sites had turned the URL page's reader away (a 403 and a 410 to anything that doesn't connect as a browser does), and getting past such a guard by imitating a browser isn't built. This is the other way: the page is opened in a real browser, by the owner's click, and the video is fetched by that browser.

- **Backup first:** the tag `backup/before-browser-window-2026-10-18` (on GitHub, at 2a9717b: `git checkout` it to go back), and a zip of the app as it was built then, kept outside the repository in a backups folder beside it.
- **The window** (`BrowserWindow.swift`): WebKit (WKWebView, as Safari is), with a private store kept in memory only, so nothing of the pages (history, cookies, sign-ins) is kept once it's shut; back, forward, reload and an address; a page's own downloads aren't made, and a page's new windows open in it. The owner sees the page and answers whatever it asks (an age check, a "not a robot").
- **Where the video is looked for** (`BrowserScript.finder`, run in every frame from the start of each page): the players (`<video>`, `<source>`, their `data-` attributes), everything the page asks for with fetch or XMLHttpRequest and what those answer (a player's own answers, JSON included, read for addresses), everything it has loaded (the resource list), its details (og:video, twitter:player:stream, JSON-LD), its links, its code (inline scripts and the page, addresses written plainly, escaped as `\/` or `/`, or percent-encoded) and its player's settings kept on the window; and streams it builds itself (MediaSource) are noticed. It looks as the page loads, as it plays, every few seconds for a minute, and again on Search Again. A request for DRM keys, or encrypted media, is noticed and said: that video is never saved.
- **What's found** (`MediaLinks`, `PageFindings` in the Kit): one entry an address, with every place it was found; a stream's parts left out (segment endings and names, byte ranges, and runs of numbered files in one folder); streams first. Each stream's list is read once, from the frame it was found in (`HLSList`: sizes, sound apart, header and byte-ranged parts; `DASHList`: templates with numbers, times and timelines, lists of parts, a whole file, the first period alone), and its sizes offered with how big each is about; a size's own list, also found, isn't listed again. A locked stream (an HLS key, a DASH ContentProtection) or a live one is said, and has no Download.
- **Saving it:** Download has the window fetch it: a stream's parts in turn from the page's own frame (as its player fetches them, with its cookies), each handed to the engine before the next is fetched; a whole file by the browser's own download, into the one file the engine names for it. The engine (`browser.start`, `browser.part`, `browser.progress`, `browser.done` in `fetch`) writes the pieces into `fetched/`, joins a picture and its sound with ffmpeg (copying: MP4, else Matroska), checks it's a video, converts it if Settings says, and keeps it as the URL page's are, in Videos, Movies, Series, Anime or 18+. Its progress is in the window, and in Downloads (18+ on Videos → 18+ alone) while the window is open; shutting the window while one is being saved asks first, and stops it.
- **Open in Browser** is on the URL page when its reader is turned away or finds nothing, and beside its list of what it did find; never for the music service's pages or SoundCloud's.
- **What's held to** (CLAUDE.md, the contract, `ENGINE_API.md`): nothing pretends to be a browser (no imitated connection, no changed user agent); nothing the window fetched is handed to another program to fetch again (no cookies passed on); nothing protected against copying is saved; nothing of YouTube's or SoundCloud's is taken there; not in a child's profile. The privacy policy says the window shows pages as any browser does and keeps nothing once shut.
- **Checked:** 10 new engine tests (`test_browser_window.py`: a stream's pieces put together and kept as an MP4 with picture and sound, a picture and its sound sent apart and joined, a whole file kept byte for byte, noise and nothing refused, pieces out of order or of no track refused, YouTube's, SoundCloud's and anything not on the web refused, Stop and a window gone quiet letting go of what came, no room, `browser.*` over RPC, a child's profile) and 9 new Kit tests (addresses found however written, what an address is, findings put together, HLS sizes and parts with ranges and a header, locked and live lists, DASH templates with timelines, a whole file, a list of parts, a protected manifest). **Unseen, in a scratch home, with made-up pages on the loopback address:** a player that builds its stream's address in code and fetches it was found by its requests alone, offered at 720p and 360p, and saved to Videos as a 720p MP4 with sound; a plain `<video>` file was downloaded by the window itself into Movies, the same byte for byte; a DASH manifest written escaped in a player's settings was found in the page's code, its requests and the settings, its picture and sound joined, and saved to 18+; a stream locked with a key was found and refused as protected.
- **Not tried:** a real site, by hand (the owner's to try: the window is theirs to browse); a site whose player fetches its parts from another site that doesn't let the page fetch them (the window says the page wouldn't fetch it); a stream the page builds only from pieces it decrypts or never names (nothing to find); a save carried on after the window is shut (it isn't).

2026-10-18, later. **The browser window's videos, the best first, with how good and how big each is** (the owner, after trying it: "Is there any way to diferentiate which of the download links has the highest quality? And if so, sort by highest quality first. Showing how big the file is"). A stream's sizes were already read from its list; a plain file's picture size wasn't known at all. Now the window has the browser read each file's details as it does before playing a video (a player made and never shown, told to load only those: `BrowserScript.measure`), one at a time: its picture's height and its length; where it can't, its height is read from its name ("_1080p", "/720/", "1920x1080": `MediaLinks.heightNamed`). Its size is what the site says it is. Each line under a video's name now says "1080p · 1.2 GB · 42:10" (a stream's size "about", reckoned from its bit rate and length: `MediaQuality`), the list is sorted the sharpest first, then the biggest, then the longest, with what can't be saved at the end (`MediaLinks.ranked`), and the first is marked **Highest quality**. **Checked:** 3 new Kit tests (heights read from addresses, the words, the order: 232 in all); unseen, a page with two plain files whose names say nothing and a stream: the browser read 720p and 360p for the files, with 93 KB and 66 KB and 0:04, and listed the stream (720p) first and marked, then the 720p file, then the 360p one.

2026-10-18, night. **The browser window stopped saving a site's streams ("the page wouldn't fetch it"), and was slow** (the owner: "It worked on the website twice. and then it stopped working", and "The bear media browser, is very slow. Extremely slow and glitchy when scrolling up and down on the options. and using the actual browser is also very slow. Please smooth it out.").

- **Fetching:** the window asked for a stream's parts with the page's cookies. A video site often keeps a stream's parts on another server that lets any page read them, which a browser allows only for a request **without** cookies, and players send none; so the browser read the stream's list but refused every part. Reproduced with made-up servers on the loopback address (the list on one that takes cookies, the parts on one that lets anyone read them): the same words, the part asked three times and never read. Now a part is asked as players ask it first (`credentials: same-origin`), then with the page's cookies, and a piece of a file, if refused, as the whole part; and fetched without forbidding the browser's own cache (a part the player has just fetched may be there). Then: saved. The words now say what the site did: "the site wouldn't let the page fetch it (…)" with the browser's own reason, and for 403 or 410 that the video's address may have run out, to play it on the page again and then Download. A whole file the browser downloads is looked at as it starts: an error, or a web page in the video's place, stops it at once and says so (it was saved, then refused as "isn't a film").
- **Speed:** the list beside the page was sorted once for every row it drew (each row asked which was the best, and each asking sorted the whole list), and every row was made, seen or not: now it's sorted once when something changes (`PageBrowser.ranked`, `best`) and only the rows in view are made (`LazyVStack`). On the page, the search ran in full every three seconds for a minute (every element's attributes, the whole page's text, and every setting the page keeps on its window turned into text), and every answer the page got (its scripts and pages too, up to 3 MB each) was copied and read: now a light look (the players, the page's details, what it has loaded) at its start, as it plays and every five seconds for forty; the full one once it has loaded and ten seconds later; the page's settings only on Search Again; and only answers that are data (JSON, under 1 MB) are read. A page's files have their sizes asked at most 24 times and their pictures read at most 12.
- **Checked:** unseen, in a scratch home: the reproduction saved (the parts server asked once, without cookies); the earlier pages as before (a hidden stream, a plain file, a DASH manifest in a player's settings, a locked stream refused, the best-first list). A 500 KB page with 151 videos on it: the app was busy 0.7 s in all over the 20 s after opening it (the longest at once 0.24 s, the window being made), and not at all over the next 10; 12 files had their pictures read. **Not checked:** scrolling and browsing by hand (the owner's to feel), and the owner's site itself.

2026-10-18. **Continue Watching Series puts the series watched last first** (the owner: "The latest series ive been watching doesnt show up at the start of the list. It should always be the one that was last watched."). The owner's records showed why: the series they'd watched an episode after another all of the 16th and 17th (each next one starting by itself) wasn't in the row at all. The row held only a series part way through an episode, as a film is; between episodes, the one just played to its end (past nine tenths) or the next just started (its length not known yet), it didn't count, so the series being watched was hardly ever there. Two smaller faults could also put it behind others: a series played again from the row was remembered with the time it was last watched before, and a sync with a paired video app or a Stremio import, which go by that time, moved it back; and where a film had got to updated its time without moving it in the list.
- **The row** (`WatchHistory.continuing`, `AppModel.continuingSeries`): a series part way through an episode, and one between episodes that has an episode played to its end and isn't caught up with its latest episode out (`SeriesProgress`), the one watched last first. One opened for a moment with nothing of it watched, and one caught up, aren't in it. Continue Watching Movies is as it was.
- **A card between episodes**, its episode played to its end, opens the next episode on its series' page with its ways to play showing (`openEpisode`), or the series' page when its episodes haven't been read yet, instead of playing the finished one again. A card at an episode not begun plays it, as before.
- **The history is kept in the order things were watched:** played again, it's stamped as watched now; where it has got to brings it to the front (`place`); read back, it's put in order of when each was watched.
- **Checked:** 3 new Kit tests (the series watched last first: between episodes, the next started by itself, after a reopen, played again from the row, after syncs with a paired video app older and newer, and gone once caught up; a history kept out of order; a Kitsu series' ids); `swift test` 235 tests and `swift build` pass. Unseen, with a copy of the owner's watching records in the test copy's own settings (put back after): Continue Watching Series starts with the series watched on the 17th, then the rest newest first; its cards look as before. The row has more in it than before (series left between episodes, further along it).

2026-10-18, later. **Continue Watching plays a series' next episode** (the owner chose it, from the earlier change's follow-up). A series card between episodes, its episode played to its end, opened the next episode on its series' page; now a click plays it, as the player's Next Episode button does: by the owner's rule (`NextEpisode`: the same quality from the same plugin, else the next plugin with that quality), through the same code (`playEpisode(after:while:)`). A click, so a torrent is joined. Where it can't be played that way, its page opens with its ways to play showing, as before; with no episode after it out, the series' page; and one whose way to play isn't known (from a sync or an import) opens the next episode's page.
- While its ways to play are found the card shows a small spinner (the one the player's Next Episode button has, on a dark square over the picture). A later click on a card, or anything else played or opened meanwhile, takes its place, and the episode isn't played (`startingEpisode`, `isStillWanted`).
- In the Kit: `WatchHistory.Entry.isBetweenEpisodes(finished:)` (played to its end here, or watched elsewhere, and not being watched again part way) and `played` (the plugin and quality it was played by).
- **Checked:** a new Kit test of both; `swift build` and `swift test` pass; the spinner pictured unseen (a temporary line, taken out after). **Not tried:** a real click on a card, which plays a film: it's the owner's to try.

2026-10-18, later still. **New Episodes on Home** (the owner: "bring this feature of sorting new episodes to the desktop app. And add the new episodes feature to home. Watchlist stays how it is."). The video app on a tablet has counted a series' new episodes since 2026-10-17 ("+1, +2": the episodes out since the owner last had none new to watch, that they haven't watched); the Mac now counts them the same way, and Home has a row of them.
- **The count** (`SeriesProgress`: `seen`, `fresh`, `freshAt`, `newEpisodes`, `withFreshEpisodes`, the tablet's own rule and tests): what's out after the latest episode seen when the last reading found nothing new left to watch; specials aren't counted; episodes already out when the owner was part way through aren't new. Kept with each series' record, which a series read before this is given at its next reading: those are due at once, once (`isDue`), so the row fills the first time Home is opened.
- **Home → New Episodes** (`series.newEpisodes`, put once on the owner's Home before the films' Continue Watching rows, `HomeLayout.addNewEpisodes`; on a new Home page too): those series, the one whose latest came out last first, each card with its "+N" in the corner. A click plays the episode to carry on with (`NextEpisode.toCarryOn`: the one last played if it isn't watched, as the next one started by itself and left before it was begun; else the one after it if it isn't watched; else the first new one), by the owner's rule, as Continue Watching's does; one left part way plays on from there; one never played here opens on that episode's page. View More opens the Watchlist. Reading the series' episodes when Home opens (every six hours) is what finds them.
- **Continue Watching Series** leaves out a series with new episodes: it's in New Episodes instead, and back in Continue Watching once they're watched. Its cards between episodes now play the episode to carry on with by the same rule.
- **The Watchlist is as it was** (the owner's word): its NEW EPISODE mark and its To Watch keep their own rule (a series caught up with that has an episode since: `hasNewEpisode`, `withNewEpisodes`, unchanged).
- **Checked:** 8 new Kit tests (the count, from the tablet's; the newest first; a record from before; the episode to carry on with; New Episodes out of Continue Watching and back; the row put on Home once): 244 in all, and `swift build`. Unseen, with a copy of the owner's records (put back after): all 334 of their series were read; none has an episode out since they caught up, so the row says so; with a made-up "+2" on one series, it was in New Episodes with its "+2" and gone from Continue Watching Series.
- **Corrected the same night**, seen in the tablet app's simulator: a series whose next episode had started by itself and been left before it was begun was carried on from the episode after that one; it's that one now (`toCarryOn`, a Kit test). Continue Watching's cards between episodes go by the same rule.

2026-10-18, night. **Films from torrents that froze or never started; a standard language; Back to a show's page; the top bar and the window's buttons over a film** (the owner's list, with pictures: a film's controls at 0:00 saying "98% here", the top bar's buttons over a film, a label "S1 E1 · FIRST PERIOD" over the picture).

- **The freeze** ("this can also stall the media player showing black screen until it says 100% here", "it gets stuck … a lot", "Pressing next episode, and using the top source currently gives a black screen that doesnt load ever", "Noticed that the ipad works fine for these files"). Reproduced with a test season made for it: three MKV episodes with Turkish sound marked as theirs, English beside it, and two sets of subtitles, as the owner's episodes are. They were seeded on this Mac only, with nothing from the internet, and played through the engine's own torrent player in the app's own film player. Switching the sound to English froze the picture for good, every time. mpv's log said why. At a film's first jump (a change of sound track, a click on the bar, or opening part way through, as Continue Watching does), it reads the MKV's cues from the film's last bytes, then asks for the place it's going on the same connection. The engine's film address had always closed each connection after one answer but never said so. FFmpeg 8.1, inside the player, keeps such a connection to ask again on, met a closed one, took the film for ended, and stopped. Every answer now says `Connection: close`. The video app on a tablet hands its player a torrent's bytes without an address, which is why it was fine there.
- **A torrent made both ways (v1 and v2) never played at all.** libtorrent's `info_hash()` names such a torrent by its v2 id cut short, so the pieces the player asked for were never matched to the film. This was found with the first test torrent (libtorrent 2.1 makes them both ways); the owner's are all v1. It's matched by the v1 id it was joined by now (`_v1`).
- "Checking what's here…" says how far it has looked ("… 35% looked through"), so a long look is seen to move. Measured on the way: a torrent's details came as quickly started the engine's way as the tablet app's (5 to 9 seconds for Sintel through the DHT alone, each way twice), so that's unchanged.
- **The standard language** ("Add a standard language in settings that is set to play every episode, for example English will be the standard from any Video,Series, Etc. Not any other language, subtitled too"). Settings → Player → Language: English as standard, 29 languages, or "Each film's own" (how films played before). Every film, episode and video plays its sound in that language when it has it. Its subtitles are only ever in that language, never another. Before, it was the film's own choice, which for the owner's episodes was Turkish sound and the first of seven sets of subtitles. The film's menus still change either, for that film. The video app on a tablet has the same setting.
- **Back from an episode goes to its show's page** ("When playing a show, and pressing back it always takes you to the shows page. Currently if we press continue playing, and then back, it takes you to the last screen"). The ✕ opens the series' page at that episode, whatever it was played from, and so does the last episode out ending (`leaveFilm`). A film goes back to where it was opened from, as before.
- **The top bar over a film** ("when the player is on, it shouldnt have the Sidebar,Customize,Search,Visualizer"). It had shown over every film since 2026-10-17. The loading screen's own rule at the window's outermost view said "show" whenever the bear wasn't up, and it overruled the film's "hide" further in. Now there's one rule at the outermost view (`AppModel.topBarAway`): away for the bear, a film, a picture with the whole screen, and the big cover.
- **The window's three buttons in full screen** ("When in fullscreen it should have the 3 button, exit, minimize, maximise, only when the mouse is moved and disappears"). They're drawn on the picture where macOS's own would be (macOS's are in the strip that's kept hidden), and come and go with the controls (`FullScreenWindowButtons`). Red closes the window, stopping the film first. Yellow leaves full screen and goes to the Dock (macOS's own does nothing in full screen). Green leaves full screen. The same over a video or the visualizer with the whole screen. A window closed while a film plays stops the film too, which was left playing unseen before.
- **"S1 E1 · FIRST PERIOD" over the picture** ("it reads information behind the video"). It was the tooltip of the Continue Watching card that had just been clicked, from Home under the film. macOS shows a view's tooltips wherever it lies, under whatever covers it, until the view is hidden. So the page under a film, or a full-screen picture, is now hidden while it's covered, and back as it was after (`PageUnderPicture`).
- **Checked:**
  - `pytest`, including 2 new tests (each answer says its connection closes, then closes it; a torrent made both ways is matched by its v1 id), and `ruff`.
  - The Sintel check: read over its address at 1.5 MB/s, every range on a connection of its own, 494 parts, 0 wrong.
  - 4 new Kit tests (what the player is told for a language); `swift build`, and `swift test` (248).
  - In the unseen copy with the test season. Before, the sound switch froze the film at 15.8 s and 22.2 s, and the next episode's switch at 23.0 s. After, these all played on: a sound switch, a subtitles switch, a jump ten seconds on, the next episode opened part way through, and a sound switch there. The sound came up English and the subtitles English by themselves.
  - The top bar's buttons were hidden under a film (shown before the change), and the page under it was hidden and came back after.
  - `MUSICORG_BENCH`: `openfilm` takes several films, and web addresses; new `sound=` and `subs=` steps; the `film` line says what's in the top bar and whether the page under the film is hidden.
